=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and two integers, k and limit. Your task is to find a non-empty subsequence of nums that:

Has an alternating sum equal to k.
Maximizes the product of all its numbers without the product exceeding limit.

Return the product of the numbers in such a subsequence. If no subsequence satisfies the requirements, return -1.
The alternating sum of a 0-indexed array is defined as the sum of the elements at even indices minus the sum of the elements at odd indices.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,3], k = 2, limit = 10
Output: 6
Explanation:
The subsequences with an alternating sum of 2 are:

[1, 2, 3]

Alternating Sum: 1 - 2 + 3 = 2
Product: 1 * 2 * 3 = 6


[2]

Alternating Sum: 2
Product: 2



The maximum product within the limit is 6.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [0,2,3], k = -5, limit = 12
Output: -1
Explanation:
A subsequence with an alternating sum of exactly -5 does not exist.

Example 3:

Input: nums = [2,2,3,3], k = 0, limit = 9
Output: 9
Explanation:
The subsequences with an alternating sum of 0 are:

[2, 2]

Alternating Sum: 2 - 2 = 0
Product: 2 * 2 = 4


[3, 3]

Alternating Sum: 3 - 3 = 0
Product: 3 * 3 = 9


[2, 2, 3, 3]

Alternating Sum: 2 - 2 + 3 - 3 = 0
Product: 2 * 2 * 3 * 3 = 36



The subsequence [2, 2, 3, 3] has the greatest product with an alternating sum equal to k, but 36 > 9. The next greatest product is 9, which is within the limit.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 150
0 <= nums[i] <= 12
-10^5 <= k <= 10^5
1 <= limit <= 5000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:
def maxProduct(nums,k,limit):
    max_sum = sum(nums)
    if k < -max_sum or k > max_sum: maybe still zero? If k outside possible alternating sum range, no subsequence. But if zero? max_sum nonnegative. Return -1. However if k outside [-max_sum,max_sum], no subsequence. Need include zero? max_sum includes zeros no effect. Good.
    S = 2*max_sum + 1
    off = max_sum
    pos = [[0]*S for _ in range(2)]
    over = [[False]*S for _ in range(2)]
    zero = [[False]*S for _ in range(2)]

    for x in nums:
        # old arrays
        old_pos = [row[:] for row in pos]? Need old for all states before taking x.
        old_over = [row[:] for row in over]
        old_zero = [row[:] for row in zero]
        # We will update pos/over/zero with taking x from old and starting singleton.
        # skip is already in current arrays? If we set new arrays = old arrays, yes. But if we update in-place using old snapshots, skip remains.
        # Let's create new arrays initialized to old.
        new_pos = [row[:] for row in pos] # skip
        new_over = [row[:] for row in over]
        new_zero = [row[:] for row in zero]
        # start singleton:
        # parity 1, sum x (offset + x), product x (or zero)
        add_singleton(new_pos,new_over,new_zero, x)
        # extend from old states:
        for p in 0,1:
           for idx in range(S):
              # if old state reachable somehow
              if old_pos[p][idx] > 0 or old_over[p][idx] or old_zero[p][idx]:
                  s = idx - off
                  np = 1-p
                  ns = s + x if p==0 else s - x
                  nidx = ns + off
                  if 0 <= nidx < S:
                      if x == 0:
                          new_zero[np][nidx] = True
                      else:
                          # from positive
                          q = old_pos[p][idx]
                          if q:
                              prod = q * x
                              if prod <= limit:
                                  if prod > new_pos[np][nidx]: new_pos[np][nidx] = prod
                              else:
                                  new_over[np][nidx] = True
                          # from over
                          if old_over[p][idx]:
                              new_over[np][nidx] = True
                          # from zero
                          if old_zero[p][idx]:
                              new_zero[np][nidx] = True
        pos,over,zero = new_pos,new_over,new_zero
    # answer
    ans = 0
    for p in 0,1:
       ans = max(ans, pos[p][k+off])
    if ans > 0: return ans
    # if no positive valid, check zero
    for p in 0,1:
       if zero[p][k+off]:
           return 0
    return -1

Pseudo full:

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        total = sum(nums)
        if k < -total or k > total:
            return -1
        offset = total
        S = 2*total + 1
        # positive DP
        dp0 = [0]*(limit+1)
        dp1 = [0]*(limit+1)
        # zero DP
        any0 = any1 = 0? Need separate variables.
        any_dp = [0,0]
        zero_dp = [0,0]

        for x in nums:
            # zero DP update using old
            nany0, nany1 = any_dp[0], any_dp[1]? But if assign and then modify, need copy.
            nany = any_dp[:]  # list of two ints, okay
            nzero = zero_dp[:]
            # start singleton
            nany[1] |= 1 << (offset + x)
            if x == 0:
                nzero[1] |= 1 << offset
            # extend
            for p in (0,1):
                bits_any = any_dp[p]
                if bits_any:
                    if x == 0:
                        nany[1-p] |= bits_any
                        nzero[1-p] |= bits_any
                    else:
                        shifted = (bits_any << x) if p == 0 else (bits_any >> x)
                        nany[1-p] |= shifted
                        bits_zero = zero_dp[p]
                        if bits_zero:
                            shifted_zero = (bits_zero << x) if p == 0 else (bits_zero >> x)
                            nzero[1-p] |= shifted_zero
            any_dp, zero_dp = nany, nzero

            # positive DP update
            if x > 0:
                ndp0 = dp0[:]  # skip
                ndp1 = dp1[:]
                if x <= limit:
                    ndp1[x] |= 1 << (offset + x)
                max_prod = limit // x
                # local refs
                old0 = dp0; old1 = dp1
                for prod in range(1, max_prod + 1):
                    b0 = old0[prod]
                    if b0:
                        ndp1[prod * x] |= b0 << x
                    b1 = old1[prod]
                    if b1:
                        ndp0[prod * x] |= b1 >> x
                dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1
            # if x==0, dp unchanged

        # answer positive
        target = offset + k
        for prod in range(limit, 0, -1):
            if (dp0[prod] >> target) & 1 or (dp1[prod] >> target) & 1:
                return prod
        # zero
        if (zero_dp[0] >> target) & 1 or (zero_dp[1] >> target) & 1:
            return 0
        return -1

Invariant after processing some prefix:
For each product p (1..limit) and parity r (0 even length,1 odd length), dp[r][p] has bit at index offset+s set iff there exists a non-empty subsequence of processed prefix with no zero? Actually product positive, so no zero. But if nums processed include zeros, positive DP ignores zeros, so subsequences with no zero. Product exactly p, length parity r, alternating sum s. Since product positive, all selected elements >0. Good.
Transition for current x>0:
- Skip: old subsequences remain, copied.
- Start new subsequence: product x, parity1, sum x. If x<=limit, set bit.
- Extend old subsequence by x:
   If old parity 0, x is at odd position (even index) so sum +x, new parity1, product p*x. If p*x<=limit, set.
   If old parity1, x at even index so sum -x, new parity0.
Because we use old dp, each subsequence uses x at most once. This enumerates all subsequences: either x not chosen (skip) or chosen as first (start) or chosen after a previous subsequence (extend). Since order is original, any subsequence either doesn't include current x or includes it; if includes, removing x leaves a subsequence of previous prefix (possibly empty). Empty handled by start. Thus complete. Product positive and <=limit; if product exceeds limit, not stored because cannot become <=limit by multiplying by positive numbers later (only zero, handled separately). Need justify "cannot become <=limit" for positive final: If product >limit and we only multiply by positive integers (remaining nums >0), product stays >limit. If a zero is selected later, product becomes 0, handled by zeroDP. Thus ignoring >limit in positive DP is safe for positive answers. Good.

ZeroDP invariant:
After processing prefix, any_dp[r] bit s iff exists non-empty subsequence (any product, including zero) of prefix with parity r, sum s. zero_dp[r] bit s iff exists such subsequence containing at least one zero.
Transition:
- Skip: copied.
- Start singleton: any; zero if x=0.
- Extend old any by x: if x=0, new subsequence contains zero, so any and zero; sum unchanged because +0/-0, parity toggles. If x>0, any shifted; if old zero, zero shifted. This enumerates all. Good.

Else -1. Since any valid subsequence either has positive product (no zero) or zero product (contains zero). If positive product >limit, invalid due limit. If product 0, valid because limit>=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a valid subsequence with product 0 and alternating sum k but zeroDP maybe not find because any_dp not updated for x>0 from old zero
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        total = sum(nums)
        if k < -total or k > total:
            return -1

        offset = total

        # Positive-product DP.
        # dp0[p] / dp1[p]: bitset of alternating sums for subsequences
        # with even/odd length and positive product exactly p (p <= limit).
        dp0 = [0] * (limit + 1)
        dp1 = [0] * (limit + 1)

        # Zero-product reachability.
        # any_dp[parity]: bitset of sums for any non-empty subsequence.
        # zero_dp[parity]: bitset of sums for subsequences containing at least one 0.
        any_dp = [0, 0]
        zero_dp = [0, 0]

        for x in nums:
            # ---------- Update zero-containing DP ----------
            nany = any_dp[:]
            nzero = zero_dp[:]

            # Start a new subsequence with current element.
            nany[1] |= 1 << (offset + x)
            if x == 0:
                nzero[1] |= 1 << offset

            for p in (0, 1):
                bits_any = any_dp[p]
                if not bits_any:
                    continue

                if x == 0:
                    # Taking 0 does not change the sum, but toggles parity.
                    nany[1 - p] |= bits_any
                    nzero[1 - p] |= bits_any
                else:
                    shifted = (bits_any << x) if p == 0 else (bits_any >> x)
                    nany[1 - p] |= shifted

                    bits_zero = zero_dp[p]
                    if bits_zero:
                        shifted_zero = (bits_zero << x) if p == 0 else (bits_zero >> x)
                        nzero[1 - p] |= shifted_zero

            any_dp, zero_dp = nany, nzero

            # ---------- Update positive-product DP ----------
            if 0 < x <= limit:
                ndp0 = dp0[:]
                ndp1 = dp1[:]

                # Start a new subsequence with current element.
                ndp1[x] |= 1 << (offset + x)

                # Extend old subsequences.
                max_prod = limit // x
                for prod in range(1, max_prod + 1):
                    b0 = dp0[prod]
                    if b0:
                        ndp1[prod * x] |= b0 << x

                    b1 = dp1[prod]
                    if b1:
                        ndp0[prod * x] |= b1 >> x

                dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1

        target = offset + k
        mask = 1 << target

        # Prefer the largest positive product not exceeding limit.
        for prod in range(limit, 0, -1):
            if (dp0[prod] & mask) or (dp1[prod] & mask):
                return prod

        # Otherwise, a zero product is valid if it exists.
        if (zero_dp[0] & mask) or (zero_dp[1] & mask):
            return 0

        return -1
```
